Scraping & Data Extraction (2026-03-24)\n   163→- **DDG package renamed**: `duckduckgo_search` is now `ddgs`. Import: `from ddgs import DDGS`. Will bite on fresh installs.\n   164→- **Regex beats LLM for structured numeric extraction**: 58% hit rate on 332 items, zero cost/latency. LLM path is backup only — regex should always be primary for prices, quantities, model numbers.\n   165→- **Rate limiting**: DDG blocks without 1-1.5s delays. `SEARCH_DELAY = 1.5` is the minimum reliable interval.\n   166→- **Dedup-before-batch**: When crawling/researching lists with repeated entries, dedup by key (e.g. `brand|model`) before making web calls, then fan results back to all matching rows. Saves API calls proportional to duplication ratio.\n   167→- **PETRONAS pricesheet format**: Headers have `*` prefix for required columns (e.g. `*Brand`, `*Category`). Header row can be anywhere in the first 25 rows. Must search all sheets — data is often not on Sheet1. Skip sheets named \"instructions\".\n   168→- **Price sanity bounds**: Filter `10 < price < 5,000,000 MYR` to catch noise (years, model numbers, phone numbers misparse as prices).\n   169→#### crawler\n#### learnings.md\n# Crawler Learnings\n\n## Web Scraping & Data Extraction (2026-03-24)\n- **DDG package renamed**: `duckduckgo_search` is now `ddgs`. Import: `from ddgs import DDGS`. Will bite on fresh installs.\n- **Regex beats LLM for structured numeric extraction**: 58% hit rate on 332 items, zero cost/latency. LLM path is backup only — regex should always be primary for prices, quantities, model numbers.\n- **Rate limiting**: DDG blocks without 1-1.5s delays. `SEARCH_DELAY = 1.5` is the minimum reliable interval.\n- **Dedup-before-batch**: When crawling/researching lists with repeated entries, dedup by key (e.g. `brand|model`) before making web calls, then fan results back to all matching rows. Saves API calls proportional to duplication ratio.\n- **PETRONAS pricesheet format**: Headers have `*` prefix for required columns (e.g. `*Brand`, `*Category`). Header row can be anywhere in the first 25 rows. Must search all sheets — data is often not on Sheet1. Skip sheets named \"instructions\".\n- **Price sanity bounds**: Filter `10 < price < 5,000,000 MYR` to catch noise (years, model numbers, phone numbers misparse as prices).\n#### mistakes.md\n# Import: `from ddgs import DDGS`.\n- **Query specificity is critical**: Generic queries return noise. Strategy: most-specific first (`brand model price Malaysia MYR`), then progressively broaden. Early-exit when regex finds a price to avoid wasted queries.\n- **Regex extraction > LLM for price scraping**: Pure regex got 58% hit rate on 332 items with zero LLM cost/latency. Use regex as primary path for structured numeric extraction from snippets.\n- **Rate limiting is non-negotiable**: DDG blocks without 1-1.5s delays between searches. Minimum reliable interval: `SEARCH_DELAY = 1.5`.\n- **Price sanity bounds**: Filter extracted prices to `10 < price < 5,000,000 MYR`. Catches years (2024), model numbers, phone numbers parsed as prices.\n- **Dedup before research**: Many pricesheets repeat same product across rows (different quantities/locations). Dedup on `brand|model` before searching, fan results back to all matching rows.\n- **Multi-currency handling**: Always convert to MYR using fixed rates (USD=4.7, SGD=3.5, EUR=5.1, GBP=6.0, AUD=3.1). Match currency symbols with regex patterns.\n#### mistakes.md\n# Mistakes\n\n(No mistakes recorded yet.)\n\n### synthesizer\n#### learnings.md\n026-06-22 07:48] (5 steps, circuit_breaker)\n- Goal: Analyze the following cross-domain data and produce CONNECTIONS.md with at least 3 non-obvious insights and actionable i\n- Final state: # Arbos State Updated: 2026-06-15T07:25 UTC  ## Status: IDLE — completed (auto-cleared: idle output) after 1 steps  ## Last Completed: Analyze the following cross-domain data and produce CONNECTIONS.m\n### Retro [2026-06-29 07:20] (1 steps, auto_cleared_idle)\n- Goal: Analyze the following cross-domain data and produce CONNECTIONS.md with at least 3 non-obvious insights and actionable i\n- Final state: # Arbos State Updated: 2026-06-29T07:30 UTC  ## Status: COMPLETE — GOAL CLEARED  ## Phase: plan  ## Deliverables - This week, the blackout broke: iqah assigned 39 tenders on June 26 across 20+ team members (Fatin, Aiman, Munirah, Dian, Richard, Justin, Mamak, Muzaffar, etc.). Justin and mamak independently watched/unwatched tenders.\n\nBut the assignment data reveals a deeper problem:\n\n| Assigned tender status | Count | Pct |\n|----------------------|-------|-----|\n| closed | 112 | 77% |\n| new | 23 | 16% |\n| matched | 5 | 3% |\n| insufficient_data | 3 | 2% |\n| awarded | 2 | 1% |\n| analyzed | 1 | 1% |\n| **Total assigned** | **146** | |\n\n77% of all assigned tenders are closed. The 3 completed wizard sessions are all from April — nothing completed since.\n\nMeanwhile, 21 wizard sessions are abandoned vs 3 completed (7:1 ratio). Users start the bid wizard but don't finish.\n\n**Connection:**\n\nThe user blackout was the visible symptom. The underlying disease is temporal: by the time humans engage, the pipeline has already moved on. Tenders are ingested, matched, then expire during the 20-30 day gap between batch assignment sessions. The assignment burst (39 in 30 min) suggests a \"clear the queue\" pattern, not engaged deal pursuit.\n\nW25's dead-man switch recommendation would detect the gap, but the real fix is automated assignment at match time — not batch assignment weeks later.\n\n**Action items:**\n- [ ] **[CRITICAL]** Auto-assign matched tenders at match time based on entity/source routing rules, not manual batch assignment\n- [ ] **[HIGH]** Add closing-date guard to assignment UI — warn or block when tender closes within 7 days\n- [ ] **[HIGH]** Implement the dead-man switch from W25 — but target it at assignment staleness, not just login absence\n\n---\n\n### Insight 2: Etimad Resurrection Disproves W25's \"Archive It\" — But Bulk Timestamp Reveals Manual Intervention, Not Pipeline Fix (HIGH)\n\n**Evidence:**\n\nW25 concluded: \"100% of etimad tenders produce zero commercial pipeline. crawler\n#### learnings.md\n# Crawler Learnings\n\n## Web Scraping & Data Extraction (2026-03-24)\n- **DDG package renamed**: `duckduckgo_search` is now `ddgs`. Import: `from ddgs import DDGS`. Will bite on fresh installs.\n- **Regex beats LLM for structured numeric extraction**: 58% hit rate on 332 items, zero cost/latency. LLM path is backup only — regex should always be primary for prices, quantities, model numbers.\n- **Rate limiting**: DDG blocks without 1-1.5s delays. `SEARCH_DELAY = 1.5` is the minimum reliable interval.\n- **Dedup-before-batch**: When crawling/researching lists with repeated entries, dedup by key (e.g. `brand|model`) before making web calls, then fan results back to all matching rows. Saves API calls proportional to duplication ratio.\n- **PETRONAS pricesheet format**: Headers have `*` prefix for required columns (e.g. `*Brand`, `*Category`). Header row can be anywhere in the first 25 rows. Must search all sheets — data is often not on Sheet1. Skip sheets named \"instructions\".\n- **Price sanity bounds**: Filter `10 < price < 5,000,000 MYR` to catch noise (years, model numbers, phone numbers misparse as prices).\n#### mistakes.md\n# Use regex as primary path for structured numeric extraction from snippets.\n- **Rate limiting is non-negotiable**: DDG blocks without 1-1.5s delays between searches. Minimum reliable interval: `SEARCH_DELAY = 1.5`.\n- **Price sanity bounds**: Filter extracted prices to `10 < price < 5,000,000 MYR`. Catches years (2024), model numbers, phone numbers parsed as prices.\n- **Dedup before research**: Many pricesheets repeat same product across rows (different quantities/locations). Dedup on `brand|model` before searching, fan results back to all matching rows.\n- **Multi-currency handling**: Always convert to MYR using fixed rates (USD=4.7, SGD=3.5, EUR=5.1, GBP=6.0, AUD=3.1).
